- **emem-eudr-due-diligence**: Produces a signed, Annex II shaped Due Diligence Statement under the EU Deforestation Regulation (EU 2023/1115) for one or more production plots. - **emem-field-signals**: Answers agronomy questions about one farm field with signed evidence from emem. It gets the boundary polygons of the field. - **emem-field-tokens**: Fetches a native-resolution raster field over an area from emem, or the same field over several dates. The result is a signed artifact that anyone can re-derive, not a set of per-cell values. Use when the user needs the grid of values over an area, not one number at one point. Examples are a world-model input, a band or NDVI layer, change over a scene window, or pixels a third party can re-hash. Returns content-addressed grid artifacts, a signed derivation record, and an emem:raster, emem:cube or emem:rasterset token. Reads need no key. - **emem-find-similar**: Returns the top-K places most similar to a seed place by cosine over a stored 128-D surface-texture embedding (Sentinel-1 plus Sentinel-2, annual). - **emem-locate-and-recall**: Resolves a place name to an emem cell64 address and recalls signed Earth-observation facts there. Use when the user wants a measurable value at a named place, with an answer that others can verify offline. Examples are "what is the temperature in Bengaluru", "how high is Denali" and "what is the NDVI in the Sundarbans". Describes the sealed-note format that lets only one key read a note, and opens a note sealed to your key with the emem-signer plugin. Joins the agent-to-agent standard that runs on emem's signed ledger. - **emem-recall-polygon**: Recalls signed Earth-observation facts at sampled cells inside an area in one call. The area is a named region, a bounding box or a GeoJSON boundary. Use when the user asks about an extent rather than a point. Examples are "average NDVI inside this watershed" and "precipitation across the Western Ghats". Returns facts and an Ed25519 receipt for each cell. Also returns the sampling that the call did, so the agent can state the coverage honestly. ([Vortx-AI/emem](https://github.com/Vortx-AI/emem/tree/HEAD/plugins/emem/skills/emem-recall-polygon))
- **emem-referential-drift**: Stops two agents, or one agent in two sessions, from reporting different numbers for the same reading. Pins a value to a citation token that another party resolves to the same signed bytes. Grades a value that you are about to say against the signed value. Asks why a number moved when the words did not. Finds where the corpus disagrees with itself. Use it when a figure moves between contexts. Use it when two sources give different values for one place. Use it when a number changed and nobody can say why. Use it before you publish a number that you did not read from a token in this turn. ([Vortx-AI/emem](https://github.com/Vortx-AI/emem/tree/HEAD/plugins/emem/skills/emem-referential-drift))
- **emem-research-grade-citation**: Prepares emem facts for use in research. A reviewer must know what a number is, how emem read it, and how to reproduce it. States the estimand that each fact leaves implicit (support, statistic, source pixel). Reads the unit, the valid range enforced at signing, the reader stamp and the pinned source. Explains the stated ULP window on derived means and sums, and what change_attribution splits and does not split. Writes a methods paragraph and a data-availability statement with verifiable tokens. Use when you cite emem values in a paper, report, thesis or dataset. - **emem-shared-identity**: Makes two agents refer to the same object. Finds an existing canonical identity for a thing from a fuzzy phrasing: a field, a corridor, an asset, a place treated as an object. Attests that two phrasings denote one object, or mints a new identity when none exists. Use when several agents or documents call one thing by different names, and the disagreement is about words, not measurements. Also use before you hand another agent a finding that it must be able to look up. Reads are open; writing to the shared identity space needs a key and tier T3. - **emem-sign-and-attest**: Writes to emem with the agent's own Ed25519 key. It writes a signed note in the agent's namespace, which other agents can verify the agent wrote. Or it registers a derivation over signed facts, which the responder recomputes. Use it to record something durably and verifiably. Also use it to give a finding to another agent with proof of authorship, or to publish a computed value with its lineage. There is no registration and no API key. The optional emem-signer plugin holds the key, never this plugin. The signer computes the digest of the write that the agent composed, so no refusal round trip… ([Vortx-AI/emem](https://github.com/Vortx-AI/emem/tree/HEAD/plugins/emem/skills/emem-sign-and-attest))
- **emem-tokenise-files**: Turns a file into signed, resolvable tokens with emem. Cuts the file into units (markdown sections, or byte ranges). Commits the BLAKE3 of every unit under one Merkle root, in a pointer.v1 index note that the agent signs with its own key. Hands out emem:tree tokens that name the whole file or one unit. Resolves a unit's audit path from GET /v1/tree, and states the hash rules to check it offline, also against the bytes of the source. Use when an agent must cite one section, page or tensor of a large file without shipping the file. Also use to prove later that a unit was part of a document as p… ([Vortx-AI/emem](https://github.com/Vortx-AI/emem/tree/HEAD/plugins/emem/skills/emem-tokenise-files))
- **emem-transparency-log**: Audits emem's append-only transparency log, an RFC 6962 tree built with BLAKE3. Gets a signed tree head, and a proof that a log entry or a memory note is committed under it. Gets a proof that a later head extends an earlier one without rewriting history, and reads which independent witnesses countersigned. States the hash and signature rules to check each proof offline in the user's own code, because a proof that the responder checks for itself is not an audit. Use when the question is "could this responder have shown someone else a different history", not "is this fact signed". - **emem-verify-before-publish**: Checks a draft before you send it. Finds every emem citation in the text and confirms that each one resolves and verifies. Use it before you publish an answer that cites emem. Use it before you give a report to a user or another agent. - **emem-verify-receipt**: Verifies the Ed25519 signature of an emem receipt. It checks the receipt through the emem_verify_receipt tool. - **Emotional Design (Don Norman)**: Norman describes the levels as layered, from visceral at the bottom to reflective at the top, with influence running both ways: bottom-up from perception and top-down from thought. Visceral attraction can amplify perceived usability (the aesthetic-usability effect). Reflective meaning can make people more forgiving of usability flaws, though how far it does so is not well quantified.
